I end up missing pieces of what they were saying, pieces I really needed to hear.\n\nDoctor: Do you often talk more than others in social situations?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think so. I mean... I know I interrupt folks, like I just told you, but I don't really just talk and talk. I mostly just say what I need to say and that's about it. I wouldn't say I talk more than anybody else in a room.\n\nDoctor: Do you have trouble organizing tasks and finishing what you start?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I do. It's a real mess. My papers, my appointment cards, the food boxes from the pantry, and even the notes I try to keep in my phone... they just get all mixed up. I look at it all and I just can't figure out what needs doing first. Usually, I don't know where to start until everything is already late or just completely messed up.\n\nDoctor: Did you have trouble sitting still or fidgeting when you were a kid?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I did. I don't really know how I feel when my leg is bouncing or my hands are moving, I just know I have to do it.\n\nDoctor: Do you often act on impulse without thinking through the consequences?\n\nPatient: I'm not really sure about that one. I mean... I haven't really noticed myself doing things without thinking about what happens after. I just kind of go about my day and do what I need to do. So I guess I don't really have a good answer for you on that.\n\nDoctor: Do you often interrupt others when they're talking?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I do that a lot. I just... jump in with an answer before the person is even done talking. Like, I'll do it to the pastor, or my cousin, or the lady at the food pantry. I just blurt something out. And then I realize I answered the wrong thing entirely, or... or I just made them mad because I cut them off. I don't mean to, I just speak before they're finished.\n\nDoctor: Do you have trouble paying attention to what you're doing for a long time?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I do. Like... when folks are talking through things with me, like ride times, or the pantry lists, or plans over at the church. I listen good at first, but then... my mind just kind of slips off somewhere else. I end up missing pieces of what they were saying, pieces I really needed to hear.\n\nDoctor: Do you often talk more than others in social situations?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think so. I mean... I know I interrupt folks, like I just told you, but I don't really just talk and talk. I mostly just say what I need to say and that's about it. I wouldn't say I talk more than anybody else in a room.\n\nDoctor: Do you have trouble organizing tasks and finishing what you start?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I do. It's a real mess. My papers, my appointment cards, the food boxes from the pantry, and even the notes I try to keep in my phone... they just get all mixed up. I look at it all and I just can't figure out what needs doing first. Usually, I don't know where to start until everything is already late or just completely messed up.\n\nDoctor: Did you have trouble sitting still or fidgeting when you were a kid?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I did. Back when I was a boy in school, the teachers were always moving my seat around. I just... I could not stay put. I'd talk out of turn a lot, and I was always getting in trouble for jumping up out of my chair before class was even done.\n\nDoctor: As a child, did you often climb on things or run excessively?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think I did that. I mean... I know I couldn't stay in my seat, like I said, but I wasn't really one to go climbing up on furniture or running all over the place. I just didn't really do all that.\n\nDoctor: Do you often do things without thinking them through when you're by yourself?\n\nPatient: No, not really. When I'm just by myself... I don't think I do that. I mean, I know I can't sit still, but I'm not really going around doing things without thinking if it's just me at the house. I haven't really noticed anything like that.\n\nDoctor: Did you often run excessively when you were a child?\n\nPatient: I think you just asked me about that... but no, like I said a minute ago, I didn't really run around like that. I had a hard time staying in my chair, but I wasn't running all over the place.\n\nDoctor: Do you often do things on impulse when you're with other people?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think I do. When I'm around other people... I don't really just act without thinking. I mean, I know I'm restless and have a hard time sitting still, but I don't just do stuff on impulse like that. I haven't really noticed that being a problem.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Well, I came in because... I just feel like I got a motor running inside me all day. It's like, if I have to sit still—whether that's at church, out there in the clinic waiting room, or even just at home—I start feeling totally trapped. I just have to get up or do something. I just can't sit still.\n\nDoctor: Do you find it hard to sit still when you're in a quiet place?\n\nPatient: Yeah, it really is. I mean... even when I’m trying my best to be respectful and quiet, I just can't stop moving. My leg will be bouncing up and down, or my fingers are just tapping on the chair. And if I'm not doing that, I'm usually grabbing whatever is nearby and just twisting it around in my hands the whole time.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel restless when you're moving around or fidgeting?\n\nPatient: I'm not really sure about that. I haven't really noticed feeling restless while I'm actually doing the moving. It's more like... I just do it without thinking. I don't really know how I feel when my leg is bouncing or my hands are moving, I just know I have to do it.\n\nDoctor: Do you often act on impulse without thinking through the consequences?\n\nPatient: I'm not really sure about that one. I mean... I haven't really noticed myself doing things without thinking about what happens after. I just kind of go about my day and do what I need to do.
